<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
While on the surface a mixed design model might seem artificial, we have done so to ease computation in the proofs while maintaining a sensible dependence structure. Indeed, because we wish to model time-correlations, we want the correlation of $\epsilon_\ell$ and $\epsilon_k$ to depend on $|t_\ell - t_k|$. In order to avoid a random covariance matrix then, we fix the $t_\ell$'s in the design. However, to avoid accruing unnecessary approximation errors in the proofs, we assume a random design on the spatial variables.
    
    Schemes other than \eqref{eq:timedependent} can be considered as long as the empirical norm it induces concentrates suitably well around the $L^2([0, 1]\times \Omega)$ norm, see Remark \ref{rmk:whyscheme}.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
In the course of proving posterior consistency, we get a worse rate than in the iid case. However, once an estimator that converges at a sufficiently fast rate can be computed, the posterior can be shown to converge at the same rate as in the iid case. 
    In particular, the posterior contraction we show implies the existence of such estimators (e.g. the posterior mean). 

    To prove the same rate as in the i.i.d. case, one linearizes the forward map at the estimator, and constructs a penalized least-squares estimator on $\theta\in \Theta$. Because we linearize the forward map, the loss function is convex. One can then construct plug-in tests at the rate in the i.i.d. case, and hence show posterior contraction at that rate as well.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- proposition--1: body_md -->
Consider the setting of \eqref{eq:introNS} with ground truth $(\nu_0, \varphi_0)$, with $\varphi_0 \in H^\alpha(\Omega)$ for $\alpha > d/2 +1$. Moreover, assume that $\nu, \|\varphi_0\|_{H^\alpha} \leq R$ for some $R>0$. There exists constants $c, C_1, C_2$ depending on $\Omega$, $R$, $\nu_{\min}$ and an exponent $\kappa = \kappa(d, \alpha) < 2$ such that for all $N \geq 1$, there is an estimator $\hat \varphi_N$ satisfying the convergence rate, for $\beta = \frac{2(\alpha +1) - d - 2}{2(\alpha +1) + d +2}$,
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\begin{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        \mathbb P_{\nu_0, \varphi_0}^N \big(\|\varphi_0 - \hat \varphi_N\|_\infty \leq C_1 N^{-\beta/2}\big) \gtrsim 1 - \exp\big(-cN^{\frac{d+2}{2(\alpha+1) + d+2}}\big)
                                                                                                                                      
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\end{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
    while $\hat \varphi_N$ is computable within $C_2N^\kappa$ floating-point operations.
<!-- end proposition--1 -->
<!-- condition-1: body_md -->
\label{con:variancebound}
    Let $\Gamma_N$ be the covariance from \eqref{eq:setting}. Suppose that there is some constant $C_1$ independent of $N$ such that
    \[
        \|\Gamma_N\| \leq C_1,
    \]
    where $\|\cdot\|$ denotes the matrix operator norm.
<!-- end condition-1 -->
<!-- condition-2: body_md -->
\label{con:inversematrix}
    For the setup in \eqref{eq:setting} with forward map valued in $V = \mathbb R^{d_V}$ as in \eqref{eq:forwardmap}, we assume that 
    \[
        \max_{i = 1, \dots, Nd_V}\sum_{j=1}^{Nd_V} |(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}| \leq C_2,
    \]
    for some constant independent of $N$.
<!-- end condition-2 -->
<!-- condition-3: body_md -->
\label{con:inversematrix2}
    For the setup in \eqref{eq:setting}, with forward map $\mathcal G$ and vector space $V = \mathbb R^{d_V}$ as in \eqref{eq:forwardmap}, suppose the following:
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        {\begin{enumerate}} 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    [label = (\alph*)]
        \item There is a known function $A: [0, 1]\to V$ independent of all $\theta\in\Theta$ so that
            \[
                \int_{\Omega} \mathcal G(\theta)(t, x)\, dx = A(t).
            \]
                                                                                                                                            
        \item For a constant $C_2$ independent of $N$, $\operatorname{tr}(\Gamma_N^{-2}) \leq C_2N$.
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@enumerate} 
        {\end{enumerate}} 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@enumerate\endcsname[1]\relax}
<!-- end condition-3 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:VARCheck}
    Let $(\epsilon_{ij})$ be as in \eqref{eq:timedependent}, and suppose that for each $j$, $(\epsilon_{ij})_{i = 1}^T$ is a stationary Gaussian $\text{VAR}(p_j)$ process ($p_j\in \mathbb N$) with parameters $A_1, \dots A_{p_j}$ and $\Sigma_j$ as in \eqref{eq:VAR}. If we further suppose that $p_j$, and $A_1, \dots, A_{p_j}$ are uniformly bounded above entry-wise independent of $j$, and the minimum eigenvalue of $\Sigma_j$ is bounded away from $0$ independently of $j$, then Condition \ref{con:inversematrix} holds.
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:inversematrix}
    Suppose $\theta, \vartheta \in \Theta$, forward map $\mathcal G$ is as in \eqref{eq:forwardmap}, and $U >0$ are so that $\|\mathcal G(\theta)\|_\infty$, $\|\mathcal G(\vartheta)\|_\infty \leq U$. Let $p_\theta^N$ and $p_\vartheta^N$ the densities as introduced after \eqref{eq:setting}. Further, suppose that Condition \ref{con:inversematrix} \textbf{or} \ref{con:inversematrix2} holds for some constant $C_2$. Then, for some constant $C = C(C_2, U)$, we have that the KL-divergence and variation from \eqref{eq:KLFormula} satisfy
    
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
       KL(p_\theta^N, p_\vartheta^N) &\leq CN \| \mathcal G(\theta) - \mathcal G(\vartheta)\|_{L^\infty_tL^2_x}^2, \text{ and } \\
       V_{2, 0}(p_\theta^N, p_\vartheta^N) &\leq CN\| \mathcal G(\theta) - \mathcal G(\vartheta)\|_{L^\infty_tL^2_x}^2.
    
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax}
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- condition-4: body_md -->
\label{con:RKHS}
    Let $\Pi'$ be a centered Gaussian Borel probability measure with RKHS $\mathcal H$ on the linear space $\Theta$ and $\ell$-dimensional domain $\mathcal Z$ from \eqref{eq:Thetadef}.  Suppose further that $\Pi'(\mathcal R) = 1$ for some separable normed linear subspace $(\mathcal R, \|\cdot\|_{\mathcal R})$. Suppose further that $\mathcal H$ satisfies the continuous embedding
    \[ \mathcal H \subseteq H^{\alpha}_c(\mathcal Z) \text{ if }\kappa \geq 1/2, \quad \text{ or }\quad \mathcal H \subseteq H^\alpha(\mathcal Z) \text{ if }\kappa < 1/2,\]
    for some regularity parameter $\alpha > 0$.
<!-- end condition-4 -->
<!-- condition-5: body_md -->
\label{con:forwardregularity}
    Let $\mathcal G$, $V$ be from \eqref{eq:forwardmap} and $\mathcal Z$, $W$ be from \eqref{eq:Thetadef}. Suppose $\mathcal G$ is measurable, and let $\kappa \geq 0$. Suppose that for some normed linear subspace $(\mathcal R, \|\cdot\|_{\mathcal R})$ of $L^2(\mathcal Z, W)$ and all $M>0$, there exists a finite constant $U \geq 1$, and $L>0$ (that may depend on $M$) such that
    \[
        \sup_{\theta \in \Theta\cap B_{\mathcal R}(M)} \sup_{(t, x)\in Q} |\mathcal G(\theta)(t, x)|_V \leq U
    \]
    and
    \[
        \|\mathcal G (\theta_1) - \mathcal G(\theta_2)\|_{L^\infty_t L^2_x}\leq L\|\theta_1 - \theta_2\|_{(H^\kappa(\mathcal Z, W))^\ast}\text{ for all }\theta_1, \theta_2 \in \Theta\cap B_{\mathcal R}(M).
    \]
    We call $\mathcal R$ the regularization space.
<!-- end condition-5 -->
<!-- condition-6: body_md -->
\label{con:forwardmapping}
    Let $\mathcal R$, $\mathcal H$ be as defined in Condition \ref{con:forwardregularity} and \ref{con:RKHS}. 
    Suppose that there is an $a \geq 0$ such that
    \[
        \mathcal G(\mathcal H)\subseteq H^{a, a/2}.
    \]
    Furthermore, suppose that for any $M$ there exists a $U$ depending on $M$ such that
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        \mathcal G(B_{\mathcal R}(M)) &\subseteq B_{H^{6, 3}}(U), and\\
        \mathcal G(B_{\mathcal H}(M)) &\subseteq B_{H^{a, a/2}}(U).
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax}
<!-- end condition-6 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
\label{rmk:besovIsSobolev}
    We refer the reader to \textsection \ref{sec:notation} for the definition of these parabolic Sobolev spaces. The choice of an anisotropic space reflects the best regularity one can prove for solutions to parabolic PDEs. When dealing with other equations, such as hyperbolic PDEs, then one would change this condition to involve isotropic spaces. A larger choice of $a$ in this condition will ultimately yield a faster contraction rate.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:TestsExist}
    Let $\mathcal G$ be as in \eqref{eq:forwardmap}, and suppose that Condition \ref{con:RKHS} holds for some regularization space $\mathcal R$, RKHS $\mathcal H$, $\ell$-dimensional domain $\mathcal Z$ and regularity parameter $\alpha$. Suppose that Conditions \ref{con:forwardregularity} and \ref{con:forwardmapping} hold for these choices, as well as for some regularity parameter $a \geq (d+1)\lor 6$.
    
    Then, set
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation}

        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:rates}
        \delta_N = N^{-\frac{a}{2a + d +2}} \quad \text{ and }\quad \varepsilon_N = N^{-\frac{\alpha+\kappa}{2\alpha + 2\kappa + \ell}}, 
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation}

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
    and assume that $\varepsilon_N \lesssim \delta_N$. Also, with $n$, $T$ and $N = nT$ as in the setting of \eqref{eq:timedependent} and \eqref{eq:setting}, set 
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:rates2}
        N^{\frac{2d+3}{2a + d + 2}} \lesssim n \lesssim N\delta_N  \quad \text{ and }\quad \delta_N^{-1}\lesssim T \lesssim N^{\frac{2a - d - 1}{2a + d +2}},
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation}

    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}}

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
    and for some $M>0$ let $\Theta_N$ be the regularization set
    \[
         \Theta_N = \{\theta\in \mathcal R : \theta = \theta_1 + \theta_2, \|\theta_1\|_{(H^\kappa(\mathcal Z))^\ast}\leq M\varepsilon_N, \|\theta_2\|_{\mathcal H}\leq M, \|\theta\|_{\mathcal R}\leq M\},
    \]
    and suppose that $\theta_0 \in \Theta_N$. 
    If Condition \ref{con:variancebound} holds, then there are tests (indicator functions) $\phi_N$ such that for any $K\in \mathbb N$ and $M>0$, there is some $C = C(M, K)$ sufficiently large so that
    \[
        \mathbb E^N_{\theta_0}[\phi_N] \to 0, \text{ and } \sup_{\substack{\theta \in \Theta_N \\ C\delta_N < \|\mathcal G(\theta)  -\mathcal G (\theta_0)\|_{L^2}}} \mathbb E_{\theta}^N[1 - \phi_N] \leq e^{-K N\varepsilon_N^2}
    \]
    provided $N$ is large enough.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- condition-7: body_md -->
\label{con:stabilityestimate}
    Let $\mathcal G$ be as in \eqref{eq:forwardmap}, and $\mathcal R$ be as in Condition \ref{con:RKHS} and \ref{con:forwardregularity}, with ground truth $\theta_0 \in \mathcal R \cap \Theta$. For some $\eta >0$ and all $M>0$, suppose there exists a constant $L' = L'(\mathcal G, M)$ such that for all $\delta >0$ small enough, 
    \[
        \sup\{ \| \theta - \theta_0\|_{L^2} : \theta \in \Theta\cap B_{\mathcal R}(M), \|\mathcal G(\theta) - \mathcal G(\theta_0)\|_{L^2(Q)} \leq \delta\} \leq L' \delta^\eta.
    \]
<!-- end condition-7 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:Main}
    Let $\mathcal G$ be as in \eqref{eq:forwardmap}, and suppose that Condition \ref{con:RKHS} holds for some prior $\Pi'$, regularization space $\mathcal R$, RKHS $\mathcal H$, $\ell$-dimensional domain $\mathcal Z$ and regularity parameter $\alpha$. Suppose that Conditions \ref{con:forwardregularity} and \ref{con:forwardmapping} hold for these choices with some regularity parameter $a \geq (d+1)\lor 6$ and that the ground truth $\theta_0\in \mathcal R \cap \mathcal H$. In the setting of \eqref{eq:timedependent} and \eqref{eq:setting} with observations $D_N$, let $\delta_N$, $\varepsilon_N$, $n$ and $T$ be as in \eqref{eq:rates} and \eqref{eq:rates2} respectively, with $\varepsilon_N \lesssim \delta_N$.
    
    Let $\Pi_N$ be the rescaled prior as in \eqref{eq:priorrescaling}. Moreover, assume that Condition \ref{con:variancebound} holds, and one of Condition \ref{con:inversematrix} or \ref{con:inversematrix2} holds. If $\Pi_N(\cdot|D_N)$ is the posterior distribution, then for all $b >0$, we can choose $m$ large enough such that as $N\to \infty$, 

    \[
        \mathbb P^N_{\theta_0}\big(\Pi_N(\theta \in \Theta, \|\mathcal G(\theta) - \mathcal G(\theta_0)\|_{L^2(Q)} \leq m \delta_N|D_N)\leq 1 - e^{-bN\varepsilon_N^2}\big) \to 0.
    \]
    Moreover, assuming Condition \ref{con:stabilityestimate} for the current choice of $\mathcal R$ and some $\eta , L' >0$, we deduce that
    \[
        \mathbb P^N_{\theta_0}\big(\Pi_N(\theta \in \Theta, \|\theta - \theta_0\|_{L^2} \leq L'(m\delta_N)^\eta|D_N)\leq 1- e^{-bN\varepsilon^2_N}\big) \to 0.
    \]
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\label{rmk:inversematrixconditions}
Note that Condition \ref{con:inversematrix2}.b is a weaker condition than Condition \ref{con:inversematrix}. Indeed, if Condition \ref{con:inversematrix} holds, then
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*}

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    \operatorname{tr}(\Gamma_N^{-2}) &= \sum_{i, j = 1}^{Nd_V} [(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}]^2 \leq Nd_V \max_{i} \sum_j [(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}]^2 \leq Nd_V \max_{i} \bigg(\sum_j |(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}|\bigg)^2 \leq Nd_VC_2^2,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}}

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
so Condition \ref{con:inversematrix2}.b holds.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{rmk:simplifyingConds}
In the setting of \eqref{eq:timedependent}, with $M_{ij}$ and $\sigma_j^k$ as in the display after that, define $M_j \equiv M^{(N)}_j$ to be the $d_VT\times d_VT $ block matrix whose entries are
\[
    M_j =  
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@pmatrix} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@pmatrix} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@pmatrix} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@pmatrix} 
        {\begin{pmatrix}} 
        {\csname pseudo@pmatrix\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@pmatrix\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@pmatrix\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@pmatrix\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        M^j_{11} - D_j & M^j_{12} & \cdots  & M^j_{1T} \\
        M^j_{21} & M^j_{22} - D_j& \cdots  & 0 \\
        \vdots & \vdots & \ddots  & \vdots \\
        M^j_{T1} & 0 & \cdots  & M^j_{TT} - D_j
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@pmatrix} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@pmatrix} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@pmatrix} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@pmatrix} 
        {\end{pmatrix}} 
        {\csname pseudo@pmatrix\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@pmatrix\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@pmatrix\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@pmatrix\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    ,
\]
where $D_j = \operatorname{diag} ((\sigma_j^1)^2, \dots, (\sigma_j^{d_V})^2)$.
Then, set
\[
    \Gamma^{(j)} = I_{T}\otimes D_j + M_j\quad \text{ for } j = 1, \dots n,
\]
where $\otimes$ is the Kronecker product of matrices.
Finally, we recall the $d_VN \times d_VN$ matrix from \eqref{eq:setting} is given in block-diagonal form by 
\[
    \Gamma_N = \operatorname{diag}\big(\Gamma^{(1)}, \Gamma^{(2)}, \dots, \Gamma^{(n)}\big)
\]
Due to the block structure of $\Gamma_N$, we have
\[
    \Gamma_N^{-1} = \operatorname{diag}\Big((\Gamma^{(1)})^{-1}, (\Gamma^{(2)})^{-1}, \dots (\Gamma^{(n)})^{-1}\Big)
\]
Thus, to show Condition \ref{con:inversematrix} holds, it suffices to show the condition holds for all the matrices $\Gamma^{(j)}$.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:longrange}
    Let $M_j$ and $\sigma_j^k$ be as in Remark \ref{rmk:simplifyingConds}. Suppose that there are positive constants $c_1$ so that $c_1 < (\sigma_j^k)^2$ for all $j$ and $k$. Also, suppose that for some $c < c_1$, $M_j$ satisfies $\max_j \| M_j \|_{\infty \to \infty} \leq c$ for sufficiently large $T$. Then, Condition \ref{con:inversematrix} is satisfied.

    In particular, if $\max_j \|M_j\|_\infty \leq c(d_VT)^{-1}$ or $\max_j \| M_j \| \leq c (d_VT)^{-1/2}$ for sufficiently large $T$, then Condition \ref{con:inversematrix} holds.
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
We note that 
    \[
        \{ \Psi_{lk}^{\operatorname{an}} : l_x, l_t \geq L_0-1, \text{ and }k \in \mathcal K (l)\}
    \]
    form an orthonormal basis of $L^2([0, 1]\times \Omega)$. Orthonormality is clear, and we know that this basis is dense in functions of the form $\sum f_i(t)g_i(x)$ for $f_i \in C([0, 1])$ and $g_i\in C(\Omega)$ in the supremum norm, which are in turn dense in $C([0, 1]\times \Omega)$ by Stone-Weierstrass, which are dense in $L^2([0, 1]\times \Omega)$.

    Moreover, we also have that
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        V^{\operatorname{an}}_{l} &= \operatorname{span}(\Psi^{\operatorname{an}}_{jk}: \mathbb L(j) \leq l, k \in \mathcal K(j))\\
        W_l^{\operatorname{an}} &= \operatorname{span}(\Psi^{\operatorname{an}}_{jk}: \mathbb L(j) = l, k \in \mathcal K(j)).
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax}
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def:AnisotropicSpace}
    For $s > 0$, fix a smoothness $S$ so that $\phi^{\operatorname{bc}}_{lk}, \psi^{\operatorname{bc}}_{lk} \in C^r$ for a natural number $r > s$. We define the anisotropic Sobolev spaces as
    \[H^{s, \operatorname{an}} = \{ f\in L^2(Q) : \|f\|_{H^{s, \operatorname{an}}} < \infty\},\]
    where 
    \[\|f\|_{H^{s, \operatorname{an}}} = \left( \sum_{\substack{l \in \mathbb N^2\\ l_t, l_x \geq L_0 -1}} \sum_{k \in \mathcal K(l)}2^{2s\lfloor \max(l_t/2, l_x)\rfloor }|\langle f, \Psi^{\operatorname{an}}_{lk}\rangle|^2\right)^{1/2}.\]
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
The definition of these Sobolev spaces can be extended to Besov spaces $B^{s, \operatorname{an}}_{pq}$, see Definition \ref{def:AnisotropicBesov} in the appendix \textsection \ref{sec:besovIsSobolev}, and a few of these spaces will be needed for the proofs in Appendix \ref{sec:besovIsSobolev}. We also quickly note that the definition of $H^{s, \operatorname{an}}$ depends only on the spaces $V_{L_0 -1}^{\operatorname{an}}$ and $W_{j}^{\operatorname{an}}$ for $j \geq L_0$. Indeed, suppose that $f\in L^2(Q)$. If we let $f_{L_0-1}$ be the orthogonal projection of $f$ onto $V_{L_0 -1}^{\operatorname{an}}$ and $f_j$ be the orthogonal projection on to $W_j^{\operatorname{an}}$, then the following norm is equivalent to $\|\cdot\|_{H^{s, \operatorname{an}}}$:
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:atomicdecomp}
        \|f\|^2 = \sum_{j \geq L_0 -1} 2^{2sj}\|f_j\|^2_{L^2(Q)}.
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
    These wavelet norms characterize the anisotropic Sobolev spaces $H^{s, s/2}$ mentioned in Remark \ref{rmk:besovIsSobolev}.
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:besovIsSobolev}
    Let $m\in \mathbb N$, then 
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        \bigcap_{k=0}^{m} H^k([0, 1], H^{2m-2k}(\Omega)) &= H^{2m, \operatorname{an}},\\
        \bigcap_{k = 0}^{m+1} H^k([0, 1], H^{2m+1 - 2k}(\Omega)) &\subseteq H^{2m+1, \operatorname{an}},
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
    where $\subseteq$ indicates a continuous embedding, and equality indicates equivalent norms. As a consequence, we have $H^{s, \operatorname{an}} = H^{s, s/2}$ for all $s\geq 0$.
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
For the proof of this proposition, see Appendix \ref{sec:besovIsSobolev}. Henceforth (except in Appendix \ref{sec:besovIsSobolev}), we will denote $H^{s, \operatorname{an}}$ by the usual notation of $H^{s, s/2}$.
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:BesovEmbeddings}
    For $s > (d+2)/2$, we have that
    \[
        H^{s, s/2} \hookrightarrow C(Q).
    \]
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:AnisotropicApprox}
    Suppose that $\alpha \geq 0$, $u \in H^{\alpha, \alpha/2}$ and let $u_L$ be the projection of $u$ onto $V_L^{\operatorname{an}}$. Then, there is a constant $c = c(\alpha)$ so that 
    \[
        \| u - u_L \|_{L^2} \leq c\|u\|_{H^{\alpha, \alpha/2}} 2^{-\alpha (L+1)}.
    \]
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:metricentropy}
    Let $B(R) = B_{H^{s, s/2}}(R)$ be a ball of radius $R$ in $H^{s, s/2}$, and $s > (d+2)/2$. Then, 
    \[\log N(B(R), \|\cdot \|_{L^\infty}, \epsilon) \lesssim \bigg(\frac{R}{\epsilon}\bigg)^{\frac{d+2}{s}}.\]
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
Throughout this section, we will assume that $V$ is $1$-dimensional in the setting of \eqref{eq:setting}. In examples such as the Navier-Stokes equations, $V$ is indeed not $1$ dimensional, but in the vector valued case, we can estimate $u_\theta$ coordinate-wise and get the same results as in the real-valued case.
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:Mestimation}
    In the setting of \eqref{eq:timedependent}, suppose that Conditions \ref{con:forwardregularity} and \ref{con:forwardmapping} hold, with $a > (d+2)/2$, and that Condition \ref{con:variancebound} holds for some constant $C_1$.
    Let $\Theta_N$ be the regularization set from Theorem \ref{thm:smallball} for some $B$ and sequence $\varepsilon_N$, and let $\delta_N$, $\hat u$ be as in \eqref{eq:growthrates} and \eqref{eq:estimator} respectively. Also, suppose that
    \[
        n \lesssim N\delta_N\text{ and } T \gtrsim \delta_N^{-1}, \text{ and } \varepsilon_N \lesssim \delta_N.
    \]
    Then, for any $K>0$ there is a constant $c$ so that for all $\theta\in \Theta_N$, we have
    \[
        \mathbb P^N_\theta\left( \|\hat u - u_{\theta}\|^2_{(N)} + \delta_N^2\|\hat \beta\|^2_{h^a} > c\delta_N^2\right) \leq e^{-KN\delta_N^2},
    \]
    where the constant $c$ depends only on $B, C_1, K$ and $d$.
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
\label{prop:Mconcentration}
    Let $K\in \mathbb N$ be some fixed positive constant. Assume the set-up and conditions of Proposition \ref{prop:Mestimation} hold with $a \geq 6$, and 
    \[
        n \gtrsim N^{\frac{2d+3}{2a + d + 2}} \quad \text{ and }\quad T \lesssim N^{\frac{2a - d - 1}{2a + d +2}}, 
    \]
    as well as that $\theta \in \Theta_N$ and $\varepsilon_N \lesssim \delta_N$. Then there exists some threshold $N_0 \in \mathbb N$ so that for $N \geq N_0$,
    \[
        \mathbb P_{\theta}^{N}(\| \hat u - u_{\theta, L} \|^2_{L^2([0, 1]\times \Omega)} \leq 2\| \hat u - u_{\theta, L}\|^2_{(N)} + \frac{c}{T^2} ) \geq 1 - e^{-KN\delta_N^2}
    \]
    where $N_0 = N_0(K, a, d, B)$, and $c$ depends only on $B$, $C_1, K$, and $d$.
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
\label{rmk:whyscheme}
    Lemma \ref{lem:deterministicMconcentration} and Proposition \ref{prop:Mconcentration} are the reason why we specialize to the case of \eqref{eq:timedependent}, and any other specific choice of design in \eqref{eq:setting} will work provided similar facts can be established. 
    
    For the proof see the Appendix, \textsection \ref{sec:MconcentrationSupp}. With this, we can now construct tests.
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
The tests we consider are
    \[
        \phi_N = \mathbbm{1}_{\{\|\hat u - u_{\theta_0}\|^2_{L^2(Q)}> E\delta_N^2 \text{ or } \|\hat \beta\|^2_{h^a} > c\}}
    \]
    for $c$ and $E$ appropriately chosen and large enough.
    For the type one and two error, we can apply Proposition \ref{prop:Mestimation} and then Proposition \ref{prop:Mconcentration} (noting that $\varepsilon_N \lesssim \delta_N$) and \eqref{eq:biascontrol2}.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{prop:HRDEReg}
    Assume $D\in C^1(\Omega)$. For $\varphi \in H^1(\Omega)$, $D \in H^s(\Omega)$, $f \in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$ and $d \leq 3$, there exists a unique solution $u$ of \eqref{eq:introRDE}. Moreover, suppose that $\|\varphi\|_{H^\gamma}, \|D\|_{H^{\gamma}}\leq B$ for $0\leq \gamma \leq s$. Then we have
    \[
        u \in H^{\gamma + 1, (\gamma+1)/2}, \quad \text{ with }\quad \|u\|_{H^{\gamma + 1, (\gamma +1)/2}}\leq C
    \]
    where $C = C(B, f, \gamma, d, D_{\min}, \|D\|_{C^1}, S)$.
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:levelsetmap}
    The map $G$ from \eqref{eq:levelsetmap} is differentiable in the Fr\'echet sense, with derivative
    \[
        DG(D, u)[h, v] = \left(\frac{\partial v}{\partial t} - \nabla \cdot (D\nabla v) - \nabla \cdot(h\nabla u) - f'(u)v, v(0)\right).
    \]
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proposition-9: body_md -->
Let $d\leq 3$. For $\mathcal U$ as in \eqref{eq:defofU} and $F$ from \eqref{eq:levelsetmap}, the map $\mathcal G: \mathcal U\to F$ defined as in \eqref{eq:HRDEforwardmap} is $C^1$ in the Fr\'echet sense, Moreover, for any $D \in \mathcal U$, its Fr\'echet derivative at $D$ is given by
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        D\mathcal G(D) : C^1(\Omega) &\to F \\
        h &\mapsto i_h
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
    where $i_h \in F$ is the unique strong solution to the linear parabolic PDE
    \[
         
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases}

<!-- condition-8: body_md -->
\label{con:Darcy}
    Let $u$ be the solution to \eqref{eq:introRDE} for the diffusivity $D$. Suppose that 
    \[
        \inf_{x\in \Omega} \int_0^1 | \nabla u(t, x)|^2 \, dt \geq c_0
    \]
    holds for some positive constant $c_0$.
<!-- end condition-8 -->
<!-- condition-9: body_md -->
\label{con:Darcy2}
    Let $u$ be the solution to \eqref{eq:introRDE} with $D$ the diffusivity, and suppose that $\Omega' \subseteq \Omega$ is some open set such that
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        {\begin{itemize}} 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        \item $D \equiv D_{\min} + 1$ on $\Omega \setminus \Omega'$, and 
        \item For some positive constants $\mu, c_0 >0$, and time $t \in [0, 1]$,
            \[
                \inf_{x \in \Omega'} \frac{1}{2} \Delta u(t, x) + \mu |\nabla u(t,x)|^2 \geq c_0.
            \]
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        {\end{itemize}} 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[1]\relax}
<!-- end condition-9 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
Conditions \ref{con:Darcy} and \ref{con:Darcy2} are incomparable conditions, and we will show that both can be satisfied with a specific choice of $\varphi$ the initial condition depending on $D_0$ only through $D_{\min}$ and $\|D\|_{C^1}$, see Propositions \ref{prop:RDvarphi} and \ref{prop:NSvarphi}. In assuming Condition \ref{con:Darcy}, we get stability on the entire domain $\Omega$.\footnote{Indeed, for a general bounded domain $\Omega \subseteq \mathbb R^d$, we need only assume that $D_0$ is known at $\partial \Omega$.} However, we prove stability in the $L^1$ norm, which leads to a worse rate in $L^2$. In assuming Condition \ref{con:Darcy2} we have to assume more knowledge about $D_0$, but obtain a better rate.

    In the case of Condition $\ref{con:Darcy2}$ it is not possible to take any $\Omega'$ such that $\overline {\Omega'} = \Omega$. At any local maximum $x^\ast\in \Omega$ of $u(t, \cdot)$, we have $\nabla u(t, x^\ast) = 0$, and $\Delta u(t, x^\ast) \leq 0$.\footnote{And then take some $x_n\in \Omega'$ such that $x_n \to x^\ast$.}
    However, we will show that Condition \ref{con:Darcy2} can be satisfied for any $\overline V \subsetneq \Omega$ for a specific choice of $\varphi$ in Appendix \textsection \ref{sec:findingphi}.
    
    The difficulty with stability for Darcy's transport operator lies in trying to recover information about $h$ from $\nabla \cdot (h\nabla u)$ at critical points of $u$. Condition \ref{con:Darcy} translates into the condition that critical points of $u(t, \cdot)$ change with time. We show that this condition is generic in Proposition \ref{prop:opendense}.
<!-- end remark-11 -->

\label{thm:HRDEBack}
    Suppose that the Condition \ref{con:Darcy}, or respectively \ref{con:Darcy2}, is satisfied for $u_{D_1}$ where $D_1 \in \Theta$ is the true parameter, and some domain $\Omega'$ in the case of Condition \ref{con:Darcy2}. Then, for the choice of regularization space $\mathcal R = H^\beta(\Omega)$, or $H^\beta_c(\Omega')$, so that $\beta > d/2+ 4$, and
    \[
        \eta =  
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        {

        {

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@cases\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
            \frac{\beta -1}{\beta +1}\frac{\beta}{\beta + d/2 + 1} & \text{ if Condition \ref{con:Darcy} holds,} \\
            \frac{\beta -1}{\beta + 1} & \text{ if Condition \ref{con:Darcy2} holds,}
         
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@cases}

    we have that for all $M >0$, there is a constant $L'$ depending on $M, d, c_0, D_{\min}, \beta,$ and also $\mu$ in the case of Condition \ref{con:Darcy2}, such that
    \[
        \|D_1- D_2\|_{L^2(\Omega)} \leq L'\|\mathcal G (D_1) - \mathcal G(D_2) \|_{L^2(Q)}^{\eta} \quad \text{ for all }D_1, D_2 \in B_{\mathcal R}(M)\cap \Theta.
    \]
<!-- end proposition-11 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:HRDEMain}
    Assume that Condition \ref{con:Darcy} or \ref{con:Darcy2} is satisfied for the ground truth $D_0 \equiv g(\theta_0)$. 
    Suppose that a Gaussian process prior $\Pi'$ verifying Condition \ref{con:RKHS} with $\mathcal R = H^\beta(\Omega)$ or $H^\beta_c(\Omega')$ resp. for some $\beta > 4 + d/2$ and RKHS $\mathcal H \subseteq H^\alpha(\Omega)$ for some $\alpha > \beta + d/2$. Let the prior $\Pi_N$ be the image measure under $g$ of the probability measure from \eqref{eq:priorrescaling} for $\kappa = 0$. 
    Consider the forward map $\mathcal G : \Theta \to L^2(Q)$ defined in \eqref{eq:HRDEforwardmap}, and assume that $\theta_0\in H^\alpha\cap \mathcal H$ or $H^\alpha_c(\Omega')\cap \mathcal H$ resp. 

    Let $\Pi(\cdot | (Y_{ij}, X_{ij}))$ be the posterior distribution arising from observations of the model \eqref{eq:timedependent} where Condition \ref{con:variancebound} holds, and suppose one of Condition \ref{con:inversematrix} or \ref{con:inversematrix2} holds. Let $\delta_N = N^{-(\alpha+1)/(2(\alpha+1) + d +2)}$, $\varepsilon_N = N^{-\alpha/(2\alpha + d)}$. Then for all $b>0$, we can choose $m$ large enough such that as $N\to \infty$
    \[
        \mathbb P^N_{D_0} \big( \Pi_N(D\in \Theta: \|\mathcal G(D)-\mathcal G(D_0)\|_{L^2(Q)} \leq m\delta_N | D_N) \leq 1 - e^{-bN\varepsilon_N^2}\big) \to 0.
    \]
    Moreover, letting $\eta$ be as in Proposition \ref{thm:HRDEBack}, and constant $M$ large enough, we also have
    \[
        \mathbb P_{D_0}^N\big(\Pi_N(D \in \Theta: \|D - D_0\|_{L^2(\Omega)} \leq M\delta_N^\eta | D_N) \leq 1 - e^{-bN\varepsilon_N^2}\big) = o(1).
    \]
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
The proof uses Theorem \ref{thm:Main}, where the parameter space is $\Theta' = \mathcal R$ and the forward map is $\mathcal G' = \mathcal G \circ g$. To satisfy the Conditions \ref{con:forwardregularity} and \ref{con:stabilityestimate}, we use Propositions \ref{prop:HRDEForward} and \ref{thm:HRDEBack} coupled with the fact that $g$ is bi-Lipschitz on bounded sets, see the ideas in \textsection 2.1.1.3 and Proposition 2.1.5 in \cite{N}. Then, push forward the prior via the map $g$ using the fact that the Bayesian updates commute with taking the image measure to deduce the result.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- proposition-12: body_md -->
\label{prop:NSReg}
    Fix an integer $a \geq 1$. For $\varphi \in \dot H^a_\diamond(\Omega)$, $\nu \in [\nu_{\min}, \infty)$ and some forcing function $f \in \dot H_\diamond^{a-1}$. Let $u$ be a strong solution of \eqref{eq:introNS}. Suppose that $\nu, \|\varphi\|_{H^a} \leq B$. Then we have
    \[
        u\in H^{a + 1, (a+1)/2}, \quad \text{ with }\quad \|u\|_{H^{a+1, (a+1)/2}}\leq C,
    \]
    where $C = C(B, f, a, d, \nu_{\min}, S)$.
<!-- end proposition-12 -->

<!-- proposition-14: body_md -->
\label{prop:NSBack}
    Suppose we have for $i=, 1, 2$ that $\theta_i = (\nu_i, \varphi_i) \in \Theta$, and the forcing $f \neq 0$ with $f \in \dot H^\beta_\diamond$. Then for any choice of regularization space $\mathcal R = \mathcal R^\beta$ from \eqref{eq:regularizationNS} so that $\beta > 4 + d/2$, and $\eta = (\beta - 1)/(\beta + 1)$. We have that for all $M > 0$, there is a constant $L'$ depending on $M, d, \nu_{\min},$ and $f$ such that
    \[
        \|\varphi_1 - \varphi_2\|_{L^2} + |\nu_1 -\nu_2| \leq L' \|u_{\nu_1, \varphi_1} - u_{\nu_2, \varphi_2}\|_{L^2}^{\eta} \quad \text{ for all }\theta_1, \theta_2 \in B_{\mathcal R}(M).
    \]
<!-- end proposition-14 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:LaplaceNonZero}
    Suppose that the forcing function $f\in \dot H^1_\diamond$ is non-zero, and let $\theta \in H^1(\Omega)$. Let $u_{\nu, \theta}$ be the corresponding solution to \eqref{eq:introNS} with $\nu \leq M$.  Then, there exists a constant $c_0$ such that
    \[
        \int_0^1 \|A u_\theta\|_{L^2}^2 \, dt \geq c_0 > 0,
    \]
    where $c_0 = c_0(f, M, d)$.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->

Let $\delta_N = N^{-(\alpha+1)/(2(\alpha+1) + d +2)}$, $\varepsilon_N = N^{-\alpha/(2\alpha + d)}$. Then for all $b>0$, we can choose $m$ large enough such that as $N\to \infty$

<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:PosteriorGeneral}
    For each $\theta\in \Theta$, let $(\mathbb P_\theta^N)_{N \geq 1}$ be a sequence of probability laws on measurable spaces $(X_N, \mathcal F_N)$, and suppose they all have densities $p_\theta^N$ with respect to some probability measure $\mu_N$ on $X_N$. We assume $\text{Law}(D_N) = \mathbb P_{\theta_0}^N$ for some ground truth $\theta_0 \in \Theta$.

    Let $\Pi_N$ be a sequence of prior Borel probability measures on $\Theta$, and let $\Pi_N(\cdot \mid D_N)$ be the posterior probability distribution on $\Theta$ given by
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\begin{equation*}}

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        d\Pi_N(\theta| D_N) \propto p^N_\theta(D_N) d\Pi_N(\theta).
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\end{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
     
    Let $\mathcal G$ be as in \eqref{eq:forwardmap}, and assume that $\|\mathcal G(\theta_0)\|_\infty\leq U$. Suppose there is a sequence $\varepsilon_N \to 0$ such that $N\varepsilon_N^2 \to \infty$ and $N\varepsilon_N^2 \geq 1$, the sets
                  
    \[

<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:smallball}
    Suppose that Condition \ref{con:forwardregularity} and \ref{con:RKHS} hold for the forward map $\mathcal G$, regularity $\alpha >0$, some $\kappa \geq 0$, $U < \infty$, regularization space $\mathcal R$ and bounded domain $\mathcal Z \subseteq \mathbb R^{\ell}$ with smooth boundary (or $\mathcal Z  = \mathbb T^{\ell}$). Denote by $\Pi_N$ the law of the rescaled prior from \eqref{eq:priorrescaling}. Finally, suppose that either Condition \ref{con:inversematrix} or Condition \ref{con:inversematrix2} holds with the constant $C_2$. If $\theta_0 \in \mathcal H \cap \mathcal R$, then the hypotheses \eqref{eq:smallball} and \eqref{eq:regularizationset} in Theorem \ref{thm:PosteriorGeneral} hold for some $A>0$, for sequence
    \[
        \varepsilon_N = N^{-(\alpha + \kappa)/(2\alpha + 2\kappa + \ell)}, 
    \]
    and any $B$ if the regularization sets are chosen as
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:regset}
        \Theta_N = \{ \theta \in \mathcal R: \theta = \theta_1 + \theta_2, \|\theta_1\|_{(H^\kappa(\mathcal Z))^\ast} \leq M\varepsilon_N,  \|\theta_2\|_{\mathcal H} \leq M, \|\theta\|_{\mathcal R}\leq M\}, 
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
    for $M = M(B)$ sufficiently large.
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
The proof is identical to Steps 1 to 3 of Theorem 2.2.2 in \cite{N}, except that by Proposition \ref{prop:inversematrix}, we have
    \[
        \mathcal B_N \supseteq \{ \theta : \|\mathcal G (\theta) - \mathcal G(\theta_0) \|_{L^\infty_t L^2_x} \leq c(U,C_2)\varepsilon_N, \|\mathcal G(\theta)\|_\infty \leq U \}.
    \]
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- remark-13: body_md -->
By $\nabla^2 \varphi$ we denote the Hessian. Equation \eqref{eq:movingcriticalpoints} translates to some non-degeneracy condition at every critical point of $\varphi$. The idea of the proof for Lemma \ref{lem:verifyingDarcy} is that \eqref{eq:movingcriticalpoints} force the critical points of the solution to move, see Appendix \ref{sec:proofsforDarcy}, \textsection \ref{sec:verifyingDarcy} for details. 

    Lemma \ref{lem:verifyingDarcy} provides an easier to work with condition instead of Condition \ref{con:Darcy} because it is easier to verify theoretically: one can find an arbitrarily small perturbation of $\varphi$ so that \eqref{eq:movingcriticalpoints} holds for some $\delta$. See Proposition \ref{prop:RDvarphi} for details.
<!-- end remark-13 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:verifyingDarcy}
    Let $D$ be the diffusivity and $\varphi$ be the initial condition in \eqref{eq:introRDE}, with $\|D\|_{H^{\alpha}} \leq M$ for $\alpha > d/2 + 5$. Suppose that 
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        {\begin{itemize}} 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        \item $\varphi$ has isolated critical points, and
        \item there is some constant $\delta$ such that for all $x^\ast\in \Omega$, 
             
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:movingcriticalpoints}
                \nabla \varphi(x^\ast) = 0 \implies |D\nabla (\Delta \varphi) + \Delta\varphi \nabla D + \nabla^2\varphi \nabla D| \geq \delta \text{ at } x^\ast.
             
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@itemize} 
        {\end{itemize}} 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@itemize\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
    Then, $u = u_D$ satisfies Condition \ref{con:Darcy} for some constant $c_0 = c_0(\delta, M, \alpha, D_{\min}, d, f)$.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proposition-15: body_md -->
\label{prop:RDvarphi}
    Suppose that $D: \Omega \to [D_{\min}, \infty)$ and $\|D\|_{C^1(\Omega)}\leq M$. Then, there exists a function $\varphi$ depending only on $d, M, D_{\min}$ such that $\varphi$ has isolated critical points and \eqref{eq:movingcriticalpoints} holds.
<!-- end proposition-15 -->
<!-- remark-14: body_md -->
See Appendix \ref{sec:proofsforDarcy}, \textsection \ref{sec:RDvarphi} for details. We leverage the existence of a single function to show that in fact satisfying Condition \ref{con:Darcy} is generic in the initial condition, which is the subject of Proposition \ref{prop:opendense}.
<!-- end remark-14 -->
<!-- proposition-16: body_md -->
\label{prop:opendense}
    Let $D$ be the diffusivity from \eqref{eq:introRDE}, with $D\in H^\alpha$ for $\alpha > d/2 + 5$. The set of initial conditions satisfying Condition \ref{con:Darcy} for some constant $c_0$ is of second category with respect to the $C^k$ norm for $k \geq 3$ (in particular it contains an open dense set).
<!-- end proposition-16 -->
<!-- remark-15: body_md -->
Having discussed when Condition \ref{con:Darcy} holds, it is worth mentioning notable times when \textit{doesn't} hold. Indeed, when there is a steady state (i.e. constant in time) solution $u$ of \eqref{eq:introRDE}, then the stationary points of $u$ trivially do not move.

    Another case to note is that of pure eigen-modes: if we consider the case when $f \equiv c$ for some constant $c$, and $e_\lambda\in L^2(\Omega)$ is an eigenfunction of $-\nabla \cdot(D\nabla )$ with eigenvalue $\lambda > 0$, then setting $\varphi = e_\lambda$, we see
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\begin{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        u(t, x) = e^{(-\lambda + c)t}e_\lambda(x),
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\end{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
    and clearly $u$ does not have moving critical points. This suggests, as expected, that when the dynamics are degenerate we are unable to infer $D$.
<!-- end remark-15 -->
<!-- proposition-17: body_md -->
\label{prop:NSvarphi}
    Suppose that $D: \Omega \to [D_{\min}, \infty)$ and $\|D\|_{C^1} \leq M$. Then, there exists a function $\varphi$ depending only on $d, M, D_{\min}, \Omega'$ such that Condition \ref{con:Darcy2} holds for $\Omega'$ and some choice of $c_0$, $\mu$ and $t = 0$.
<!-- end proposition-17 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Take any non-zero function $f$ such that $f:\Omega \to [0, 1]$, $f \in C^\infty_c(\overline {\Omega'}^c)$. Then, there is some constant $c(d, V) > 0$ such that 
    \[
        \int (1 - cf) \, dx = 0,
    \]
    and set $g = 1 - cf$. Then $g \in \dot C^\infty(\Omega)$, so by Proposition \ref{prop:EigenSobolevChar} there is a solution $\varphi \in C^\infty(\Omega)$ such that
    \[
        g = \nabla \cdot (D\nabla \varphi).
    \]
    Now we can use an idea from \cite{NvdGW} Lemma 24, and note that for $x\in \Omega'$, 
    \[
        1 = g(x) = D(x)\Delta \varphi(x) + \nabla D(x) \cdot \nabla \varphi(x),
    \]
    so either $\Delta \varphi(x) \geq 1/(2\|D\|_\infty)$ or $|\nabla \varphi(x)|^2 \geq 1/(2\|D\|_{C^1})^2$. Thus, we can choose $\mu = \mu(M, d, V)$ so that
    \[
        \inf_{x \in \Omega'} \left[\frac{1}{2}\Delta \varphi (x) + \mu |\nabla \varphi (x)|^2\right]\geq c_0,
    \]
    where $c_0$ depends on $M, d, \Omega', D_{\min}$. Taking this $\varphi$ to be the initial condition of $u_D$ gives Condition \ref{con:Darcy2}.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
From Remark \ref{rmk:simplifyingConds}, it suffices to show that Condition \ref{con:inversematrix} holds for a general $VAR(p)$ process with a constant depending only on an upper bound of its parameters, $p$, and $d_V$. Now, if $X = (X_1, \dots X_T) \sim N(0, \Sigma)$ is a $VAR(p)$ process, then we let $Q = \Sigma^{-1}$ and $Q_{ij}$ to be $d_V\times d_V$ sub-matrices of $Q$ for $i, j = 1, \dots T$.
       
                            
                                                   
                                                 
                                                  
                                               
                       
       
    But we know from \cite{L} that the precision matrix of a stationary $VAR(p)$ process is a $p-$banded block matrix, which is Toeplitz away from the extremes:
    i.e. $Q_{ij} = 0$ for $|i-j| > p$, and $Q_{ij} = Q_{i'j'}$ when $i - j = i'-j'$ and $p< i, i',j, j' < T - p+1$. The upper bound follows from the explicit form of the matrix derived in \cite{L}, equation (3.4.4).
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
For ease of notation, let $u = \mathcal G(\theta)$ and $v = \mathcal G(\vartheta)$. We will additionally write $p_\theta^N$ as $p_\theta$. Note that 
\[
    p_\theta(y, x) \propto \exp\left( -\frac{1}{2} \sum_{ij} (y_i - u(t_i, x_i))^T(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(y_j - u(t_j, x_j)) \right),
\]
where for $i, j = 1, \dots, N$, we will denote by $(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}$ as the $d_V \times d_V$ submatrices of $\Gamma_N$. Note that $(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}^T = (\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ji}$.
Hence, after some algebraic manipulation, the log-liklihood is 
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\begin{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    \log(\frac{p_\vartheta}{p_{\theta}}) &= - \sum_{ij} \epsilon_i^T (\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij} (u(t_j, X_j) - v(t_j, X_j)) \label{eq:logliklihoodexpansion}\\
                              & \hspace{10mm}- \frac{1}{2}\sum_{ij} (u(t_i, X_i) - v(t_i, X_i))^T(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij} (u(t_j, X_j) - v(t_j, X_j)),\nonumber
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\end{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
under the distribution of $p_\theta$.
Now, let $u(t_i, X_i) - v(t_i, X_i) \equiv u_i - v_i$. Then, we observe from the trace trick and $\mathbb E_\theta [\epsilon_i] = 0$ that the KL divergence satisfies
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\begin{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    -\mathbb E_{p_{\theta}} [\log \frac{p_\vartheta}{p_{\theta}}] = \frac{1}{2}\sum_{ij} \operatorname{tr}\big[(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij} \mathbb E[(u_j - v_j)(u_i-v_i)^T]\big].\label{eq:KLExpression}
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\end{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
In the case of Condition \ref{con:inversematrix}, assume also for simplicity that $d_V = 1$. Then, use Cauchy-Schwarz to get 
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\begin{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    |KL(p_\theta, p_\vartheta)| \leq \frac{1}{2}\sum_{ij} |(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}| \|u(t_i, \cdot) - v(t_i, \cdot) \|_{L^2(\Omega)}\|u(t_j, \cdot) - v(t_j, \cdot) \|_{L^2(\Omega)}.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\end{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
Then, we apply Condition \ref{con:inversematrix} and obtain 
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:KLBound}
    KL(p_{\theta}, p_\vartheta) \lesssim N \|u - v\|_{L^\infty_tL^2_x}^2.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    

Now, we move on to the more involved bound (still assuming $d_V = 1$) on the Kullback-Leibler variation $V_{2, 0}$.
\[
    V_{2, 0}(p_{\theta}, p_\vartheta) = \mathbb E_{p_{\theta}} [\log \frac{p_\vartheta}{p_{\theta}}]^2 - KL(p_{\theta}, p_\vartheta)^2
\]
If we square and expand \eqref{eq:logliklihoodexpansion}, noting $\mathbb E_{p_\theta}[\epsilon_{i}\epsilon_{i'}] = (\Gamma_N)_{ii'}$ and $\mathbb E_{p_\theta}[\epsilon_i] = 0$, we get
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\begin{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
&= \sum_{jj'} (\Gamma_N^{-1})_{jj'}\mathbb E_{p_{\theta}}[(u- v)(t_j, X_j)(u- v)(t_{j'}, X_{j'})]\nonumber \\
& \quad +\frac{1}{4}\sum_{ii'jj'}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{i'j'}\mathbb E_{p_{\theta}}\Big[(u- v)(t_{i'}, X_{i'})(u- v)(t_{j'}, X_{j'})(u- v)(t_i, X_i)(u- v)(t_j, X_j)\Big] \nonumber \\
&\quad -\frac{1}{4}\sum_{ii'jj'}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{i'j'}\mathbb E_{p_{\theta}}[(u- v)(t_{i'}, X_{i'})(u- v)(t_{j'}, X_{j'})]\mathbb E_{p_{\theta}} [(u- v)(t_i, X_i)(u- v)(t_j, X_j)]\label{eq:KLvariationeq}
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align} 
        {\end{align}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
We can bound the first term like the KL divergence case. The second two terms cancel out whenever $X_{i'}, X_{j'}$ are independent of $X_i, X_j$, i.e. when $\{i, j\} \cap \{i', j'\} = \emptyset$. When this is not the case, at least one of the following is true: $i = i'$, $i = j'$, $j = i'$, or $j = j'$. Hence, we can bound the above sum by (using H\"older's inequality)
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
&\leq \frac{1}{2}\sum_{ijj'}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij'}\|(u-v)(t_i, \cdot)\|_{L^4_x}^2\|(u-v)(t_j, \cdot)\|_{L^4_x}\|(u-v)(t_{j'}, \cdot)\|_{L^4_x}\\
&\quad + \frac{1}{2}\sum_{ii'j}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{i'i}\|(u-v)(t_i, \cdot)\|_{L^4_x}^2\|(u-v)(t_{i'}, \cdot)\|_{L^4_x}\|(u-v)(t_j, \cdot)\|_{L^4_x}\\
&\quad + \frac{1}{2}\sum_{ijj'}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{jj'}\|(u-v)(t_i, \cdot)\|_{L^4_x}\|(u-v)(t_j, \cdot)\|_{L^4_x}^2\|(u-v)(t_{j'}, \cdot)\|_{L^4_x}\\
&\quad + \frac{1}{2}\sum_{ii'j}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{i'j}\|(u-v)(t_i, \cdot)\|_{L^4_x}\|(u-v)(t_{i'}, \cdot)\|_{L^4_x}\|(u-v)(t_j, \cdot)\|_{L^4_x}^2
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
Now, using Condition \ref{con:inversematrix}, we observe finally that
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {

        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:KLvariationBound}
    V_{2, 0}(p_{\theta}, p_\vartheta) \lesssim N (\|u- v \|_{L_t^\infty L_x^4}^2 + \|u - v \|_{L_t^\infty L_x^4}^4).
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation}

        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
Consider the case of $(a)$ in Condition \ref{con:inversematrix2} (and no longer assume $d_V = 1$). Going back to \eqref{eq:KLExpression}, we see
\[
    KL(p_\theta, p_\vartheta) = \frac{1}{2}\sum_{ij} \operatorname{tr} \big[(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}\mathbb E[(u_j - v_j)(u_i - v_i)^T]\big].
\]
But by Condition \ref{con:inversematrix2}.a, we know for $i \neq j$ that 
\[
    \mathbb E[(u_i - v_i)(u_j - v_j)^T] = \mathbb E[(u-v)(t_i, X_i)]\mathbb E[(u-v)(t_j, X_j)^T] = 0.
\]
So, plugging this into \eqref{eq:KLExpression},
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\begin{equation}}

        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    \label{eq:KLtrickery}
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@split} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@split} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@split} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@split} 
        {\begin{split}} 
        {\csname pseudo@split\endcsname[0]\relax}

        {\csname pseudo@split\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@split\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@split\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    KL(p_\theta, p_\vartheta) &= \frac{1}{2} \sum_{i} \operatorname{tr}\big[(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ii}\mathbb E[(u_i-v_i)(u_i-v_i)^T]\big]\\
    &\leq \frac{1}{2} \sum_{i}\operatorname{tr}\big[(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ii}\big] \|u-v\|^2_{L^\infty_tL^2_x} \leq \frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}(\Gamma_N^{-1})\|u-v\|^2_{L^\infty_tL^2_x},
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@split} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@split} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@split} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@split} 
        {\end{split}} 
        {\csname pseudo@split\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@split\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@split\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@split\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation}

    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation}

    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation} 
        {\end{equation}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
where in the first inequality we have used the facts that 1) for two non-negative symmetric matrices $M$ and $L$, $\operatorname{tr}(ML)\leq \operatorname{tr}(M) \|L\|$, and 2) for $w\in V$, if we set $L = ww^T$, then $L$ is non-negative symmetric matrix and $\|L\| = |w|_V^2$. For the KL-variation, we derive an expression akin to \eqref{eq:KLvariationeq} for a general $d_V$,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    V_{2, 0}(p_\theta, p_\vartheta) &= \sum_{jj'}\operatorname{tr}\big[(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{jj'}\mathbb E [(u_{j'}-v_{j'})(u_{j} - v_{j})^T]\big]\\
    +\frac{1}{4}\mathbb E\bigg[ &\sum_{ii'jj'} (u_{i}-v_{i})^T(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(u_{j}-v_{j})(u_{i'}-v_{i'})^T(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{i'j'}(u_{j'}-v_{j'})\bigg]\\
    -\frac{1}{4}\sum_{ii'jj'}&\mathbb E[(u_{i}-v_{i})^T(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(u_{j}-v_{j})]\mathbb E[(u_{i'}-v_{i'})^T(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{i'j'}(u_{j'}-v_{j'})]
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
but this time we note that second two terms cancel out whenever: a) $\{i, j\}\cap\{i', j'\} = \emptyset$, or b) there is one index distinct from the other three.
Thus, we get
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    V_{2, 0}(p_\theta, p_\vartheta) &= \sum_{j}\operatorname{tr}\big[(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{jj}\mathbb E[(u_j-v_j)(u_j-v_j)^T] \big]\\
                              &+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{ij}\mathbb E[(u_i-v_i)^T (\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(u_j-v_j)(u_i-v_i)^T(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(u_j-v_j)]\\
                              &\qquad\qquad - \mathbb E[(u_i-v_i)^T(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(u_j - v_j)]^2\\
                              &+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{ij, i\neq j}\mathbb E[(u_i-v_i)^T (\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(u_j-v_j)(u_j-v_j)^T(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ji}(u_i-v_i)]\\
                              &\qquad\qquad - \mathbb E[(u_i-v_i)^T(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(u_j - v_j)]^2.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
The first term is handled the same as in the KL-divergence case. For the other two terms, we consider the bound, for $i, j = 1, \dots N$, 
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    &\mathbb E[(u_i-v_i)^T (\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(u_j-v_j)(u_i-v_i)^T(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(u_j-v_j)] - \mathbb E[(u_i-v_i)^T(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(u_j - v_j)]^2\\
    &\quad \leq \mathbb E\big[\operatorname{tr}[(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ji}(u_i - v_i)(u_i-v_i)^T (\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(u_j - v_j)(u_j - v_j)^T]\big]\\
    &\quad \leq \mathbb E\big[\operatorname{tr}[(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ji}(u_i - v_i)(u_i-v_i)^T (\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}]|u_i - v_i|_V^2\big]\\
    &\quad \leq \operatorname{tr}[(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ji}]\mathbb E[|u_i-v_i|_V^2|u_j - v_j|_V^2]\\
    &\quad \lesssim\operatorname{tr}[(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ji}]\|u-v\|_{L^\infty_tL^4_x}^4,
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
where we have again used the facts noted after \eqref{eq:KLtrickery}.
Now, we observe via block matarix algebra that
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*}

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
\sum_{ij} \operatorname{tr}[(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ij}(\Gamma_N^{-1})_{ji}] = \operatorname{tr}(\Gamma_N^{-2}).
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\end{equation*}}

    }

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
Putting this together, we obtain
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\begin{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
    V_{2, 0}(p_\theta, p_\vartheta) \lesssim \operatorname{tr}(\Gamma_N^{-2})\|\mathcal G(\theta) - \mathcal G(\vartheta)\|_{L^\infty_tL^4_x}^4+\operatorname{tr}(\Gamma_N^{-1})\|\mathcal G(\theta) - \mathcal G(\vartheta)\|_{L^\infty_t, L^2_x}^2.
 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {

        {

        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\end{equation*}}

    }

    }

    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
Now, we conclude by noting from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, $\operatorname{tr}(\Gamma_N^{-1}) \leq \operatorname{tr}(\Gamma_N^{-2})^{1/2}N^{1/2}$,
so by condition $(b)$ in Condition \ref{con:inversematrix2}, and $\|\mathcal G(\theta)\|_\infty, \|\mathcal G(\vartheta)\|_\infty \leq U$, we are done.
